Hi... yeah, thanks. Um, so I guess what finally made me come in is that I just keep forgetting basic adult things. Like, paying my bills, showing up for appointments, following through on errands... and returning interview emails. Which is huge right now since I just finished my bachelor's and I'm supposed to be job hunting. 

Actually, last week I got an email back from this really cool company, and I was so excited because their office is right next to this bakery that makes those little matcha croissants? I am completely obsessed with matcha. My roommate says it tastes like dirt, but she literally drinks this homemade kombucha that smells like actual feet, so I don't even listen to her. We actually got into a massive argument about fridge space the other day because her jars are taking over the entire bottom shelf and she refuses to... wait, sorry, what was I saying? 

Oh, right. The interview email. I completely forgot to reply to it because I got distracted, and by the time I remembered days later, they had already moved on. It's just... stuff like that. It's starting to cost me real opportunities, and I don't know how to fix it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah. Definitely. Like, sitting still is basically torture for me. If I have to sit through an interview, or a meeting, or even just a movie... oh my gosh, movies are actually the worst. I went to see that really long sci-fi movie with my friend last weekend, and the theater we went to had those reclining seats? Which you'd think would be great, but they're covered in this weird fake leather that makes a horrible squeaking noise every single time you move. Oh, definitely. All the time. Like... okay, so when I was still doing biotech data work for my degree, I would always think a script or a spreadsheet was completely done and ready to go. But then later I'd realize I had completely mislabeled a sample ID, or just skipped a whole note in the protocol. Which is really bad in a lab setting, obviously. 

Actually, the lab I worked in always smelled like bleach and this weird... algae? We were working with these algae strains for a biofuel project, which is honestly super fascinating. Did you know that algae can produce like ten times more energy per acre than regular land crops? My lab partner, Kevin, was totally obsessed with it. He literally wore a green shirt every single day to match the algae. I asked him about it once and he called it his "algae aesthetic." Kevin was so weird. He also used to chew on the ends of his pens while we were working, and I always thought he was going to accidentally chew on a pipette one day, which is so unsanitary... wait, sorry, what was I talking about? 

Oh, right! Mistakes. Yeah, so I'd eventually submit my documentation for the project, and it would just be full of these really obvious typos that I somehow just did not see at all. Like my brain just completely glazed over them even though I swear I proofread it.

Which is really bad in a lab setting, obviously. \n\nActually, the lab I worked in always smelled like bleach and this weird... algae? We were working with these algae strains for a biofuel project, which is honestly super fascinating. Did you know that algae can produce like ten times more energy per acre than regular land crops? My lab partner, Kevin, was totally obsessed with it. He literally wore a green shirt every single day to match the algae. I asked him about it once and he called it his \"algae aesthetic.\" Kevin was so weird. He also used to chew on the ends of his pens while we were working, and I always thought he was going to accidentally chew on a pipette one day, which is so unsanitary... wait, sorry, what was I talking about? \n\nOh, right! Like my brain just completely glazed over them even though I swear I proofread it.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty with organization and focus, or did these issues start more recently?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it's pretty much always been like this. Like, even way back in elementary school. My mom actually kept all my old report cards in this massive binder. It's one of those really thick, heavy duty ones? She loves organizing things. She has a label maker that she uses for literally everything in the house. The spices, the pantry shelves, even the TV remotes. Which I guess is helpful, but she once labeled the cat's food bowl, which just felt a little excessive. The cat's name is Barnaby, by the way. He's this huge, fluffy Maine Coon mix and he sheds everywhere. I have to lint roll my clothes like three times before I leave the house, especially if I'm wearing black, because his fur is this bright orange color... wait. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, I'm not really sure? I don't think I ever really paid attention to how distracted the other kids were, so I couldn't tell you if I was worse than them. I was usually just doing my own thing, honestly. 

Like, in third grade, my best friend Chloe and I spent the entire year obsessed with making these elaborate houses out of mud and twigs by the chain-link fence during recess. We actually found this massive, shiny green beetle once while we were digging. It was iridescent, almost like a piece of jewelry? We put it in this little plastic Tupperware container that my mom had packed my grapes in. But the container still smelled like leftover spaghetti, because my mom was always sending me to school with spaghetti. Which is honestly the worst lunch, because the red sauce stains the plastic forever, you know? Like, no matter how much you scrub it, it always has that weird orange tint. And if you accidentally put it in the bottom rack of the dishwasher, the lid completely warps and never snaps on right again, so it just spills everywhere in your backpack... 

Wait. I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Did you ask about my friends in school?

Um, no, I don't think I've ever actually lost a friend over it. Like I said, my friends are pretty used to me being all over the place. I mean, maybe someone got annoyed and drifted away, but no big dramatic friend breakups or anything. 

Actually, the closest thing to a friend breakup I ever had was with this girl Sarah in middle school, but that was because she borrowed my favorite sweater and returned it with a massive bleach stain on the sleeve. Have you ever been to Vermont in the fall? It's honestly beautiful, but the traffic is insane because everyone drives up from New York and Massachusetts just to look at trees. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, fidgety as a child? I don't know about when I was a kid, honestly. But I definitely feel it now. Like, if I have to sit through a meeting, or an interview like this, or even just watch a movie, I get this intense pressure building up inside my body. It's like I physically need to get up or shift around or do something with my hands, or I'm going to crawl out of my skin. 

It's actually why I can't go to the movie theater anymore. The last time I went, I tried to watch that new superhero movie, the one with the multiverse stuff? And the seats were those fancy recliners, which you'd think would be comfortable, but they're made of this weird faux leather that squeaks every time you breathe. And the guy next to me was eating popcorn so loudly, like, crunching with his mouth open. Who does that in a quiet scene? It was driving me insane. I ended up focusing so much on his chewing that I completely missed the plot twist. And then the AC was blasting, so I was freezing, but I couldn't get up to leave because I was in the middle of the row and didn't want to be that person squeezing past everyone...

Wait, sorry. Did I answer your question? I just get really restless when I have to sit still.

Um, yeah, definitely in quiet environments. Quiet activities are so hard for me. Like, if I try to sit and read, or meditate, or just hang out calmly with friends, I can't just *be*. I end up tapping my foot, or humming, or talking, or checking my phone, instead of just being still and quiet.

Like, if it's too quiet, you can hear all the weird noises your body makes. I started wondering if I had a deviated septum or something, and then I remembered this documentary I watched about plastic surgery in South Korea, and how it's super common to get nose jobs as graduation presents over there. Which is wild, right? Like, here you get a car or a laptop, and there you get a new face. I watched it on a Tuesday night when I was supposed to be writing a paper on macroeconomics, but I ended up going down this massive YouTube rabbit hole about skincare routines instead...

Wait, sorry. What was the question? Oh, yeah, quiet settings. I just can't do them. I always end up doing something else instead of being still.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Romantic relationships? Um, no, not really. I haven't noticed any major problems with that. I mean, I haven't really dated much since I finished my degree anyway. Dating apps are just so exhausting to keep up with. Like, you have to come up with all these witty answers to prompts, and it feels like a part-time job. 

My friend Jessica met her boyfriend on one of those apps, and his whole profile was just pictures of him holding fish. Why do guys do that? Like, is the fish supposed to be impressive? I don't even like seafood. Well, except for shrimp, but only if it's fried. I went to this seafood boil place once for a birthday party, and they literally just dump all the food right on the table on this big piece of butcher paper. It's so messy! You have to wear a plastic bib, and my bib ripped, so I got garlic butter all over my favorite sweater. It was this really soft cashmere one I found at a thrift store for like five dollars. Can you believe that? Five dollars for real cashmere! I tried to get the stain out with baking soda and dish soap because I saw a hack for it on TikTok, but it just left this weird bleached spot on the sleeve, and now I can only wear it around the house...

Wait, I'm sorry. What did you ask me? Something about dating? Yeah, no, I don't really have any relationship problems right now.

It's like I physically need to get up or shift around or do something with my hands, or I'm going to crawl out of my skin. \n\nIt's actually why I can't go to the movie theater anymore. Quiet activities are so hard for me. Like, if I try to sit and read, or meditate, or just hang out calmly with friends, I can't just *be*. I end up tapping my foot, or humming, or talking, or checking my phone, instead of just being still and quiet. \n\nI actually tried meditating once because my old roommate swore by it. She had this whole setup in her room with a salt lamp and a little fountain that sounded like someone left the sink running. She told me to just focus on my breathing, but all I could focus on was how much my nose whistled when I exhaled. Have you ever noticed that? Like, if it's too quiet, you can hear all the weird noises your body makes. Um, no, not really. I haven't noticed any major problems with that. I mean, I haven't really dated much since I finished my degree anyway. Dating apps are just so exhausting to keep up with. Like, you have to come up with all these witty answers to prompts, and it feels like a part-time job. \n\nMy friend Jessica met her boyfriend on one of those apps, and his whole profile was just pictures of him holding fish. I went to this seafood boil place once for a birthday party, and they literally just dump all the food right on the table on this big piece of butcher paper. It's so messy! You have to wear a plastic bib, and my bib ripped, so I got garlic butter all over my favorite sweater. It was this really soft cashmere one I found at a thrift store for like five dollars. Um, yeah, I mean, I wasn't officially diagnosed back then, but the signs were definitely there. Like, even in elementary school, my teachers would always write on my report cards that I was bright but I constantly lost my worksheets. I'd rush through all the directions, talk out of turn, and my desk was just... a disaster zone. I could never find my assignments in there. It was like a black hole for paper.

Actually, speaking of black holes, I watched this crazy documentary about space the other night. They were talking about how time actually slows down when you get closer to a black hole because of the gravitational pull. It's called time dilation, I think? It just blew my mind. Like, if you fell in, you wouldn't even realize how much time was passing on Earth. You'd just be stretching out like spaghetti. Spaghettification! That's the actual scientific term for it, which is so funny to me. I made spaghetti the next night because I couldn't stop thinking about it, but I forgot I put the garlic bread in the oven and it turned into literal charcoal. The smoke alarm went off, and I had to open all the windows, but it was raining, so then my couch got wet... 

Wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, before I was 12. Yeah, my teachers were always getting onto me about my messy desk and talking too much. So, definitely had the symptoms back then.

What were we talking about? \n\nOh, right, my report cards. Yeah, so my mom kept them all, and every single year, the teachers would write the exact same thing. They'd say I was bright, but I was constantly losing my worksheets, or I'd just completely rush through the directions without reading them. And I was always talking out of turn. Plus, my desk was an absolute disaster zone. Like, it was so messy I could never find my assignments when it was time to turn them in. So yeah, it's definitely not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity are causing significant problems in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they really are. It's actually why I'm here. Like... okay, so I got fired from my biotech job recently, which was a whole disaster. And since then, I've been trying to get a new job, obviously. But I keep missing interview follow-ups. Like, I'll get an email, tell myself I'll reply in five minutes, and then I just... don't. \n\nI actually did that with this one company that seemed really cool. They make these biodegradable packing peanuts out of mushrooms? Or like, mycelium or whatever. Which is amazing because regular packing peanuts are the absolute worst. They get everywhere, they stick to everything with static electricity, and they're terrible for the environment. I ordered a lamp online once and the box was literally 90% packing peanuts. I was finding them under my couch for months. And my cat, Barnaby—the orange one I mentioned?—he kept trying to eat them, which I'm pretty sure is toxic. So I had to run around the apartment sweeping them up in an absolute panic... \n\nWait, sorry. The interview follow-ups. Right. Yeah, so I missed that email, and I lost the opportunity. And I also had to step back from this volunteer project I really cared about because I just couldn't keep track of the schedule or the emails. So now I'm completely unemployed, and I honestly just feel like I can't reliably manage the basic steps of moving my life forward right now. It's just really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity caused problems in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think so? Like, I haven't noticed it causing huge blowouts or anything. My friends know I'm just kind of scatterbrained. We actually have this group chat called \"The Chaos Coordinator\" which is mostly just them reminding me about things. We've been friends since freshman year of college. We all lived on the same floor in this dorm that had the absolute worst heating system. In the winter, it would be freezing, but then randomly in April, it would just blast heat like a sauna. We had to keep our windows wide open just to survive. One time a bird actually flew into our room because the window was open, and it took us like three hours to get it out. My roommate tried to lure it with a piece of bread, but it just kept flying into the ceiling fan... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, relationships. Yeah, no, I don't think it's been a big problem with my family or friends. They're pretty understanding, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Were you more inattentive or easily distracted as a child compared to your peers?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure? I don't think I ever really paid attention to how distracted the other kids were, so I couldn't tell you if I was worse than them. I was usually just doing my own thing, honestly. \n\nLike, in third grade, my best friend Chloe and I spent the entire year obsessed with making these elaborate houses out of mud and twigs by the chain-link fence during recess. We actually found this massive, shiny green beetle once while we were digging. It was iridescent, almost like a piece of jewelry? We put it in this little plastic Tupperware container that my mom had packed my grapes in. But the container still smelled like leftover spaghetti, because my mom was always sending me to school with spaghetti. Which is honestly the worst lunch, because the red sauce stains the plastic forever, you know? Like, no matter how much you scrub it, it always has that weird orange tint. And if you accidentally put it in the bottom rack of the dishwasher, the lid completely warps and never snaps on right again, so it just spills everywhere in your backpack... \n\nWait. I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Did you ask about my friends in school?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost a friend because of your difficulties with organization, focus, or impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I've ever actually lost a friend over it. Like I said, my friends are pretty used to me being all over the place. I mean, maybe someone got annoyed and drifted away, but no big dramatic friend breakups or anything. \n\nActually, the closest thing to a friend breakup I ever had was with this girl Sarah in middle school, but that was because she borrowed my favorite sweater and returned it with a massive bleach stain on the sleeve. It was this really soft, oversized gray cardigan that I wore literally every single day. I got it at a thrift store in Vermont when my family went leaf-peeping one October. Have you ever been to Vermont in the fall? It's honestly beautiful, but the traffic is insane because everyone drives up from New York and Massachusetts just to look at trees. My dad got so mad because we were stuck on this two-lane highway behind a tractor for like forty-five minutes, and he refused to turn the AC on because he said it \"wasted gas,\" so we just sat there sweating...\n\nWait, sorry. No, I haven't lost any friends because of my focus issues. Just the sweater thing.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with organization, focus, or impulsivity ever caused you significant problems in your relationships with family members?\n\nPatient: Um, my family? Not really, I don't think. I mean, my mom gets frustrated sometimes when I forget to call her back, but she knows I'm not doing it on purpose. It's just that I'll see her text and think, \"Oh, I'll call her right after I finish this,\" but then I completely forget. \n\nLike last week, she texted me while I was trying to organize my bookshelf. I had this sudden urge to color-code all my books, which sounded like a great idea at the time. Have you ever tried that? It looks so aesthetically pleasing, like a rainbow on your wall. But then you realize you can't actually find anything because you don't remember what color the spine of *The Great Gatsby* is. I ended up with all my books just piled on the floor for three days. My cat absolutely loved it, though. She made this little nest in the middle of all the paperbacks and refused to move. She’s a rescue, a calico, and she has this weird obsession with paper. Like, if you leave a receipt on the counter, she will find it and shred it into a million tiny pieces. I had to stop buying tissue paper for gifts because she would just dive into the bags...\n\nWait, sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, my family. Yeah, no, they don't really get that mad at me. They're pretty used to it by now.\n\nDoctor: Were you more fidgety or restless as a child compared to other kids your age?\n\nPatient: Um, fidgety as a child? I don't know about when I was a kid, honestly. But I definitely feel it now. Like, if I have to sit through a meeting, or an interview like this, or even just watch a movie, I get this intense pressure building up inside my body. It's like I physically need to get up or shift around or do something with my hands, or I'm going to crawl out of my skin. \n\nIt's actually why I can't go to the movie theater anymore. The last time I went, I tried to watch that new superhero movie, the one with the multiverse stuff? And the seats were those fancy recliners, which you'd think would be comfortable, but they're made of this weird faux leather that squeaks every time you breathe. And the guy next to me was eating popcorn so loudly, like, crunching with his mouth open. Who does that in a quiet scene? It was driving me insane. I ended up focusing so much on his chewing that I completely missed the plot twist. And then the AC was blasting, so I was freezing, but I couldn't get up to leave because I was in the middle of the row and didn't want to be that person squeezing past everyone...\n\nWait, sorry. Did I answer your question? I just get really restless when I have to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with focus and impulsivity are more prominent in certain settings, like at work or in quiet environments?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, definitely in quiet environments. Quiet activities are so hard for me. Like, if I try to sit and read, or meditate, or just hang out calmly with friends, I can't just *be*. I end up tapping my foot, or humming, or talking, or checking my phone, instead of just being still and quiet. \n\nI actually tried meditating once because my old roommate swore by it. She had this whole setup in her room with a salt lamp and a little fountain that sounded like someone left the sink running. She told me to just focus on my breathing, but all I could focus on was how much my nose whistled when I exhaled. Have you ever noticed that? Like, if it's too quiet, you can hear all the weird noises your body makes. I started wondering if I had a deviated septum or something, and then I remembered this documentary I watched about plastic surgery in South Korea, and how it's super common to get nose jobs as graduation presents over there. What was the question? Oh, yeah, quiet settings. I just can't do them. I always end up doing something else instead of being still.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with organization, focus, or impulsivity ever led to problems in your romantic relationships?\n\nPatient: Romantic relationships? Um, no, not really. I haven't noticed any major problems with that. I mean, I haven't really dated much since I finished my degree anyway. Dating apps are just so exhausting to keep up with. Like, you have to come up with all these witty answers to prompts, and it feels like a part-time job. \n\nMy friend Jessica met her boyfriend on one of those apps, and his whole profile was just pictures of him holding fish. You have to wear a plastic bib, and my bib ripped, so I got garlic butter all over my favorite sweater. It was this really soft cashmere one I found at a thrift store for like five dollars. Yeah, no, I don't really have any relationship problems right now.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms significantly interfered with your ability to function in daily life?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean, it's definitely messing things up for me right now. My neighbor has this tiny Chihuahua-terrier thing that literally lunges at my ankles every time I check my mail. And the mailboxes are in this weird little alcove where there's no escape, so I have to do this weird dance to avoid getting bitten. The mail carrier actually started leaving my packages at the front office because she didn't want to deal with the dog either, which means I have to walk all the way to the leasing office to get my stuff. And the leasing agent is always trying to get me to renew my lease early, even though I don't even know if I'll have a job...\n\nWait, sorry. Did I answer your question? I just feel like I can't get anything done right now.\n\nDoctor: Were you diagnosed with attention-deficit/hyperactivity disorder, or did you exhibit noticeable symptoms of it, before the age of 12?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean, I wasn't officially diagnosed back then, but the signs were definitely there. Um, as a kid? I don't really remember running around or climbing on things a lot. I wasn't really a playground kid, you know? But I do get this feeling now. We actually have this group chat called \"The Chaos Coordinator\" which is mostly just them reminding me about things. We've been friends since freshman year of college. We had to keep our windows wide open just to survive. One time a bird actually flew into our room because the window was open, and it took us like three hours to get it out. Yeah, no, I don't think it's been a big problem with my family or friends. They're pretty understanding, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Were you more inattentive or easily distracted as a child compared to your peers?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure? I don't think I ever really paid attention to how distracted the other kids were, so I couldn't tell you if I was worse than them. I was usually just doing my own thing, honestly. \n\nLike, in third grade, my best friend Chloe and I spent the entire year obsessed with making these elaborate houses out of mud and twigs by the chain-link fence during recess. We actually found this massive, shiny green beetle once while we were digging. It was iridescent, almost like a piece of jewelry? We put it in this little plastic Tupperware container that my mom had packed my grapes in. But the container still smelled like leftover spaghetti, because my mom was always sending me to school with spaghetti. Which is honestly the worst lunch, because the red sauce stains the plastic forever, you know? Like, no matter how much you scrub it, it always has that weird orange tint. And if you accidentally put it in the bottom rack of the dishwasher, the lid completely warps and never snaps on right again, so it just spills everywhere in your backpack... \n\nWait. I'm sorry, what were we talking about? I mean, maybe someone got annoyed and drifted away, but no big dramatic friend breakups or anything. \n\nActually, the closest thing to a friend breakup I ever had was with this girl Sarah in middle school, but that was because she borrowed my favorite sweater and returned it with a massive bleach stain on the sleeve. It was this really soft, oversized gray cardigan that I wore literally every single day. I got it at a thrift store in Vermont when my family went leaf-peeping one October. Have you ever been to Vermont in the fall? It's honestly beautiful, but the traffic is insane because everyone drives up from New York and Massachusetts just to look at trees. My dad got so mad because we were stuck on this two-lane highway behind a tractor for like forty-five minutes, and he refused to turn the AC on because he said it \"wasted gas,\" so we just sat there sweating...\n\nWait, sorry. It's like I physically need to get up or shift around or do something with my hands, or I'm going to crawl out of my skin. \n\nIt's actually why I can't go to the movie theater anymore. I just get really restless when I have to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your difficulties with focus and impulsivity are more prominent in certain settings, like at work or in quiet environments?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, definitely in quiet environments. Like, I'll get an email, tell myself I'll reply in five minutes, and then I just... don't. \n\nI actually did that with this one company that seemed really cool. It's just really frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with organization, focus, and impulsivity caused problems in your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really think so? Like, I haven't noticed it causing huge blowouts or anything. My friends know I'm just kind of scatterbrained. We actually have this group chat called \"The Chaos Coordinator\" which is mostly just them reminding me about things. We've been friends since freshman year of college. We all lived on the same floor in this dorm that had the absolute worst heating system. In the winter, it would be freezing, but then randomly in April, it would just blast heat like a sauna. We had to keep our windows wide open just to survive. One time a bird actually flew into our room because the window was open, and it took us like three hours to get it out. My roommate tried to lure it with a piece of bread, but it just kept flying into the ceiling fan... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, relationships. Yeah, no, I don't think it's been a big problem with my family or friends. I don't think I ever really paid attention to how distracted the other kids were, so I couldn't tell you if I was worse than them. I was usually just doing my own thing, honestly. \n\nLike, in third grade, my best friend Chloe and I spent the entire year obsessed with making these elaborate houses out of mud and twigs by the chain-link fence during recess. We actually found this massive, shiny green beetle once while we were digging. It was iridescent, almost like a piece of jewelry? We put it in this little plastic Tupperware container that my mom had packed my grapes in. But the container still smelled like leftover spaghetti, because my mom was always sending me to school with spaghetti. Which is honestly the worst lunch, because the red sauce stains the plastic forever, you know? Like, no matter how much you scrub it, it always has that weird orange tint. And if you accidentally put it in the bottom rack of the dishwasher, the lid completely warps and never snaps on right again, so it just spills everywhere in your backpack... \n\nWait. It was this really soft, oversized gray cardigan that I wore literally every single day. I got it at a thrift store in Vermont when my family went leaf-peeping one October. Have you ever been to Vermont in the fall? It's honestly beautiful, but the traffic is insane because everyone drives up from New York and Massachusetts just to look at trees. No, I haven't lost any friends because of my focus issues. Just the sweater thing.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with organization, focus, or impulsivity ever caused you significant problems in your relationships with family members?\n\nPatient: Um, my family? I end up tapping my foot, or humming, or talking, or checking my phone, instead of just being still and quiet. \n\nI actually tried meditating once because my old roommate swore by it. Um, yeah, it's actually been really bad lately. Since getting fired, I just feel like I cannot reliably manage the basic steps of moving my life forward. Like, I've completely missed interview follow-ups, which is why I'm still unemployed. 

I actually bought this really nice blazer for those interviews. It's navy blue with these vintage-looking gold buttons. The whole lobby always smells like old carpet and, for some reason, cabbage. I don't know which of my neighbors is constantly boiling cabbage, but it just lingers. I tried putting one of those plug-in air fresheners in the hallway, but it just made it smell like lavender and cabbage, which is honestly so much worse. I read once that lavender is supposed to reduce anxiety, so I bought a lavender pillow spray, but my cat hated it and wouldn't sleep on my bed for a week. He's a rescue, and he's super picky about smells. If I even open a jar of pickles, he runs out of the room...

Wait, sorry, what was the question? Oh, if it's causing me distress. Yeah, definitely. Because of all this, I even had to step back from a volunteer project that I really cared about. I just couldn't keep up with it. So, yeah, it's impacting basically everything right now.

Which is honestly the worst lunch, because the red sauce stains the plastic forever, you know? Like, no matter how much you scrub it, it always has that weird orange tint. And if you accidentally put it in the bottom rack of the dishwasher, the lid completely warps and never snaps on right again, so it just spills everywhere in your backpack... \n\nWait. It was this really soft, oversized gray cardigan that I wore literally every single day. I got it at a thrift store in Vermont when my family went leaf-peeping one October. Have you ever been to Vermont in the fall? It's honestly beautiful, but the traffic is insane because everyone drives up from New York and Massachusetts just to look at trees. Just the sweater thing.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with organization, focus, or impulsivity ever caused you significant problems in your relationships with family members?\n\nPatient: Um, my family? Not really, I don't think. I mean, my mom gets frustrated sometimes when I forget to call her back, but she knows I'm not doing it on purpose. It's just that I'll see her text and think, \"Oh, I'll call her right after I finish this,\" but then I completely forget. \n\nLike last week, she texted me while I was trying to organize my bookshelf. But then you realize you can't actually find anything because you don't remember what color the spine of *The Great Gatsby* is. I ended up with all my books just piled on the floor for three days. Um, severe enough to affect my daily interactions? No, I don't think so. I mean, my family gets annoyed sometimes if I forget to do a chore or something, but it's not like a huge deal that ruins our relationship.

I made spaghetti the next night because I couldn't stop thinking about it, but I forgot I put the garlic bread in the oven and it turned into literal charcoal. Yeah, my teachers were always getting onto me about my messy desk and talking too much. So, definitely had the symptoms back then.\n\nDoctor: Did you exhibit excessive running, climbing, or physical restlessness as a child?\n\nPatient: Um, as a kid? I don't really remember running around or climbing on things a lot. I wasn't really a playground kid, you know? But I do get this feeling now. Like, if I have to sit through a job interview, or a meeting, or even just sitting in a movie theater, I get this intense pressure inside my body. It's like I have to get up, or shift in my seat, or at least do something with my hands, otherwise I feel like I'm literally going to crawl out of my skin. \n\nSpeaking of movie theaters, I went to see that new superhero movie last week, and the seats were those fancy recliners? Like, they look like small lions. I saw a video of one playing in the snow, and it was just bounding through these massive drifts. I've always wanted to go somewhere with real snow, like Colorado or something. I've only ever seen flurries here. But I don't know if I could handle the cold. I'm always freezing. I have this one heated blanket that I basically live under from November to March. It has a timer on it, which is good, because otherwise I'd probably fall asleep and roast myself. I got it on sale at Target, and they had all these different colors, but I ended up getting this weird plaid pattern because it was the only one left... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Like, I've completely missed interview follow-ups, which is why I'm still unemployed. \n\nI actually bought this really nice blazer for those interviews. It's navy blue with these vintage-looking gold buttons. But when it arrived, the mail carrier had just shoved the package into my tiny apartment mailbox. My building is super old, so the mailboxes are those tiny metal slots from like the 1970s. The whole lobby always smells like old carpet and, for some reason, cabbage. I tried putting one of those plug-in air fresheners in the hallway, but it just made it smell like lavender and cabbage, which is honestly so much worse. I read once that lavender is supposed to reduce anxiety, so I bought a lavender pillow spray, but my cat hated it and wouldn't sleep on my bed for a week. No, I don't think so. I mean, my family gets annoyed sometimes if I forget to do a chore or something, but it's not like a huge deal that ruins our relationship. \n\nMy mom did get pretty mad at me last Thanksgiving, though, because I was supposed to bring the cranberry sauce and I completely forgot. But honestly, canned cranberry sauce is the best kind anyway. You know how it slides out of the can and still has the ridges on it? It's so satisfying to slice. My aunt tries to make it from scratch every year with real cranberries and orange zest, but it always turns out super bitter. She's really into baking, too. She made this three-tier chocolate cake for my cousin's graduation, and it was covered in fondant. Have you ever eaten fondant? It looks pretty, but it tastes like sweet play-dough. I tried making my own play-dough once when I was a kid using flour and salt, but I put way too much food coloring in it and my hands were stained blue for like three days. I looked like a Smurf.
